  hydra.core.coders.LanguageFeature = union{

  ## The target runtime can invoke an n-ary function with fewer than n arguments without error. When absent, the emitter eta-expands partially-applied terms.
  partialApplication: unit,

  ## The target runtime can handle deeply nested case statements without stack issues. When absent, the emitter hoists cases out into top-level helpers.
  nestedCaseStatements: unit,

  ## The target language permits polymorphic let-bindings in expression position. When absent, the emitter hoists polymorphic lets to top level.
  nestedPolymorphicLetBindings: unit}@{
  description = "A target-language or target-runtime capability the coder may rely on. Absence from a Language's supportedFeatures set means the emitter must work around the missing capability."}

  ## The name bound to the matched variant's payload (from the original handler lambda)
  parameter: hydra.core.model.Name,

  ## The lowered handler body for the matched variant's payload
  handler: hydra.core.coders.TcoStep}@{
  description = "A single alternative of a TCO-lowered case statement, mirroring Core.CaseAlternative. The original handler is always a lambda binding the variant's payload; 'parameter' preserves that bound name so coders know what to bind when printing the branch."}
  hydra.core.coders.TcoCaseStatement = record{

  body: hydra.core.coders.TcoStep}@{description = "A set of 'let' bindings together with a lowered body, mirroring Core.Let"}

  ## Parameter names, in order; these are the loop's mutable loop variables

  hydra.core.coders.TcoStep = union{

  ## Exit the loop, yielding this value

  `cases`: hydra.core.coders.TcoCaseStatement,

  `let_`: hydra.core.coders.TcoLet}@{description = "One node of a TCO-lowered function body: a loop continuation, a terminal value, or a branch"}

  ## The host-independent declarative metadata for the primitive: name, description, signature, totality and purity flags, and an optional reference implementation.
  definition: hydra.core.packaging.PrimitiveDefinition,

  ## A concrete, host-specific implementation of the primitive function: given the current graph, a mapping from a list of argument terms to a result term, or an error. Arguments are reduced and stripped of annotations by the interpreter before the implementation is invoked, so the implementation can pattern-match the argument terms directly; a higher-order primitive whose result shape is fixed by its data argument can return an unreduced applicative term and let the outer reducer fold it (no graph needed). The graph is retained for the higher-order primitives that must evaluate a function argument mid-computation (e.g. lists.filter): evaluating that function resolves primitive names against the graph's primitive table. The legacy `InferenceContext` parameter was vestigial and has been removed (https://github.com/CategoricalData/hydra/issues/446).
  implementation: hydra.core.graph.Graph → list<hydra.core.model.Term> → either<hydra.core.errors.Error, hydra.core.model.Term>}@{

  hydra.core.parsing.ParseInput = record{

  ## The number of codepoints consumed so far, i.e. the absolute offset of the first remaining codepoint
  offset: int32,

  ## The offset just past the most recently completed token, excluding any whitespace or comments that followed it. Used to give source spans a tight end
  tokenEnd: int32,

  ## The remaining unparsed input, as codepoints. Represented as a list rather than a string, so that consuming one character is a constant-time operation
  codepoints: list<int32>}@{
  description = "The input state threaded through a parser: the unconsumed codepoints, together with the number of codepoints already consumed, and where the last token ended. The offset makes the absolute source position of any parse step available in constant time, without rescanning the input."}
  hydra.core.parsing.ParseResult = (forall a. union{

  ## A successful parse, with a value and the remaining unparsed input
  success: hydra.core.parsing.ParseSuccess a,

  ## A failed parse, with an error message and the remaining input
  failure: hydra.core.parsing.ParseError})@{description = "The result of a parse operation"}
  hydra.core.parsing.ParseSuccess = (forall a. record{

  ## The parsed value
  value: a,

  ## The remaining unparsed input
  remainder: hydra.core.parsing.ParseInput})@{description = "A successful parse result"}
  hydra.core.parsing.Parser = (forall a. wrap(hydra.core.parsing.ParseInput → hydra.core.parsing.ParseResult a))@{
  description = "A parser which consumes characters from a codepoint list and produces a value. The input is a list, rather than a string, so that consuming one character is a constant-time operation rather than a linear-time string copy."}

  hydra.core.regex.Alternation = list<hydra.core.regex.RegexSequence>@{
  description = "A non-empty list of alternative sequences, joined by the | operator. Matches if any alternative matches."}
  hydra.core.regex.Atom = union{

  ## A literal character, matched exactly. The character is a Unicode scalar value in the range [U+0000, U+10FFFF] (surrogates excluded), held as an int32 code point (the range fits in signed 32-bit, and int32 matches Hydra's string/parser code-point representation). In concrete syntax, a metacharacter is written escaped (e.g. \.); escaping is purely a concrete-syntax concern, so the AST holds the bare code point.
  literal: int32,

  ## The . metacharacter; matches any single character, INCLUDING newline (unlike most host engines, whose native . excludes newline). To exclude newline, write [^\n] explicitly.
  any: unit,

  ## The ^ anchor; matches the empty string at the start of input.
  anchorStart: unit,

  ## The $ anchor; matches the empty string at the end of input.
  anchorEnd: unit,

  ## A parenthesized sub-expression, ( ... ); groups an alternation for quantification.
  group: hydra.core.regex.Alternation,

  ## A character class, [ ... ]; matches any single character in (or, if negated, not in) the set.
  class: hydra.core.regex.CharacterClass}@{description = "A single, unquantified regex element: the smallest unit to which a quantifier may apply."}
  hydra.core.regex.CharacterClass = record{

  ## True for a negated class ([^ ... ]), which matches any character NOT listed.
  negated: boolean,

  ## The class members: individual characters and/or character ranges. Must be non-empty; the empty class [] and negated-empty class [^] are excluded from the core (they are not in the POSIX/PCRE/ECMA intersection). hydra.core.parse.regex rejects an empty class.
  items: list<hydra.core.regex.ClassItem>}@{description = "A bracketed character class, [ ... ] or [^ ... ]."}
  hydra.core.regex.CharacterRange = record{

  ## The first character of the range (inclusive), as an int32 Unicode scalar value.

  ## A single character (int32 Unicode scalar value) in the class.

  hydra.core.regex.Quantified = record{

  one: unit,

  oneOrMore: unit,

  atLeast: int32,

  hydra.core.system.StatusCode = wrap(int32)@{
  description = "A process exit status: the value passed to the POSIX exit() function and reported by wait() / waitpid() for a normally-terminated child (XSH; see https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/functions/exit.html and https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/functions/wait.html). By the convention of XCU section 2.8.2, Exit Status for Commands (https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/utilities/V3_chap02.html#tag_19_08_02), 0 denotes success and non-zero denotes failure.   decode: list<a> → list<a>})@{description = "A pair of inverse permutations on lists, used to relate two orderings of the same elements"}
  hydra.core.topology.TarjanState = record{

  ## Next available index for vertices in the DFS traversal
  counter: int32,

  ## Mapping from vertices to their indices in the DFS traversal
  indices: map<hydra.core.topology.Vertex, int32>,

  ## Mapping from vertices to their lowest reachable index in the DFS traversal
  lowLinks: map<hydra.core.topology.Vertex, int32>,

  ## Current DFS stack, with vertices in reverse order
  stack: list<hydra.core.topology.Vertex>,

  ## Set of vertices currently on the stack, for quick lookup
  onStack: set<hydra.core.topology.Vertex>,

  ## Accumulated strongly connected components, each a list of vertices
  sccs: list<list<hydra.core.topology.Vertex>>}@{description = "State carried by Tarjan's strongly connected components algorithm during a depth-first traversal"}

  hydra.core.typing.FunctionStructure = (forall env. record{

  ## Type parameters (from type lambdas)

  ## Value parameters (from lambdas)

  ## Let bindings accumulated from the term

  hydra.core.typing.InferenceContext = record{

  ## Counter used to generate distinct fresh type variables during inference
  freshTypeVariableCount: int32,

  ## The current subterm-path trace, accumulated backwards (head = most-recently-pushed step, corresponding to the deepest point in the descent). At the moment an inference error is constructed, the list is reversed and wrapped into a SubtermPath (root-to-leaf order) and stamped onto the error.
  trace: list<hydra.core.paths.SubtermStep>}@{
  description = "State threaded through type inference: the fresh type variable counter and the current subterm-path trace."}

  ## The value parameters of the term, in order

  description = "A structured signature for a term: an ordered list of type parameters (with optional class constraints), an ordered list of value parameters, and a result.   hydra.core.validation.ValidationProfile = record{

  ## The set of fully qualified rule names whose findings are treated as errors. Each name has the form 'hydra.core.error.<package>.<UnionType>.<variant>', e.g. 'hydra.core.error.model.InvalidTermError.duplicateBinding'.
  errorRules: set<hydra.core.model.Name>,

  ## The set of fully qualified rule names whose findings are treated as warnings. Same name format as errorRules.
  warningRules: set<hydra.core.model.Name>,

  ## Hard upper bound on collected errors. Validation terminates as soon as the errors list reaches this length. A value of 1 reproduces the legacy 'first error wins' behaviour.
  maxErrors: int32,

  ## Soft upper bound on collected warnings. Once the warnings list reaches this length, further warning matches are silently dropped, but validation does not terminate; it continues until maxErrors is reached or the traversal completes.
  maxWarnings: int32}@{
  description = "Configuration for a validation pass: which check rules are active, how each is classified, and the upper bounds on collected findings. A check whose rule name appears in neither set is never evaluated. Errors hard-stop the traversal once maxErrors is reached; warnings only stop being collected once maxWarnings is reached, never causing termination."}
  hydra.core.validation.ValidationResult = (forall e. record{

  ## Findings classified as errors, in traversal order, bounded by the profile's maxErrors.
  errors: list<e>,

  ## Findings classified as warnings, in traversal order, bounded by the profile's maxWarnings.
  warnings: list<e>})@{
  description = "The outcome of a validation pass: an ordered list of error findings and an ordered list of warning findings, each parameterized by the finding payload type. The pass is considered successful when 'errors' is empty; warnings are informational and do not affect that judgement."}
